\left|S_{1}\right|,\left|S_{-1}\in[n/4,3n/4]\right|
.

Proof.

Since
\left|S_{1}\right|=\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathds{1}(y_{i}=1)
,

\left|S_{-1}\right|=\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathds{1}(y_{i}=-1)
,
we have
\mathbb{E}[\left|S_{1}\right|]=\mathbb{E}[\left|S_{-1}\right|]=n/2
.
By Hoeffding’s inequality, for arbitrary
t>0
the following holds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{\left|\left|S_{1}\right|-\mathbb{E}{\left|S_{1}\right|}\right|\geq t}\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{2t^{2}}{n}\right),\mathbb{P}{\left|\left|S_{-1}\right|-\mathbb{E}{\left|S_{-1}\right|}\right|\geq t}\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{2t^{2}}{n}\right).

Setting
t=\sqrt{(n/2)\log(4/\delta)}
and taking a union bound, it follows that with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|S_{1}\right|-\frac{n}{2}\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}\log\left(\frac{4}{\delta}\right)},\penalty\ \left|\left|S_{-1}\right|-\frac{n}{2}\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}\log\left(\frac{4}{\delta}\right)}.

Therefore, as long as
n\geq 8\log(4/\delta)
, we have
\sqrt{n\log(4/\delta)/2}\leq n/4
and hence
n/4\leq\left|S_{1}\right|,\left|S_{-1}\right|\leq 3n/4
.
∎

The following lemma estimates the norms of the noise vectors

\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}
for all
i\in[n]
.

Lemma C.4
.

Suppose
\delta>0
and
s=\Omega(\log(4n/\delta))
.
Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}^{2}s/2\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 3\sigma_{p}^{2}s/2,

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\leq\sigma_{p}s,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s\pm O(\sqrt{log(4n/\delta)}s^{-1/2}),

for all
i\in[n]
.

Proof.

By Bernstein’s inequality, with probability at least
1-\delta/(2n)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}-\sigma_{p}^{2}s\right|=O(\sigma_{p}^{2}\sqrt{s\log(4n/\delta)}).

Therefore, if we set appropriately
s=\Omega(\log(4n/\delta))
, we get

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}^{2}s/2\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 3\sigma_{p}^{2}s/2.

Let
k\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
. Since
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]
is Gaussian, we have
\left|\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]\right|
is sub-gaussian satisfying

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left|\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]\right|-\mathbb{E}[\left|\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]\right|]\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert\left|\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]\right|\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle=2\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma_{p}.

By sub-gaussian tail bounds, with probability at least
1-\delta/2n
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s\right|=O(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s\log(4n/\delta)}).

Therefore, if we set appropriately
s=\Omega(\log(4n/\delta))
, we get

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\leq\sigma_{p}s.

By union bound, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma C.5
.

Suppose
\delta>0
and
s=\Omega(n^{2}\log(4n/\delta))
.
Let
S_{1},S_{2}\subset[n]
satisfying
S_{1}\cup S_{2}=[n]
,
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are disjoint, and

\left|S_{1}\right|-\left|S_{2}\right|=c>0
for some constant
c
, then

\displaystyle c\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}\leq\sum_{i\in S_{1}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\sum_{i\in S_{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\leq c\sigma_{p}s.

Proof.

By sub-gaussian tail bounds, with probability at least
1-\delta/2n
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s\right|=O(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s\log(4n/\delta)}).

Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S_{1}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\geq\left|S_{1}\right|\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s-O(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s\log(4n/\delta)})\right),

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S_{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\leq\left|S_{2}\right|\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s+O(\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s\log(4n/\delta)})\right),

which imply

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S_{1}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\sum_{i\in S_{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\geq c\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s-O(n\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s\log(4n/\delta)}).

If we set appropriately
s=\Omega(n^{2}\log(4n/\delta))
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S_{1}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\sum_{i\in S_{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\geq c\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in S_{1}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\sum_{i\in S_{2}}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\leq c\sigma_{p}s.

∎

Now turning to network initialization, the following lemmas study the inner product between a randomly initialized value parameter vector
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}
for
j\in\left\{\pm 1\right\}
and
r\in[m_{v}]
or query/key parameter vector
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}
/
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}
for
s\in[m_{k}]
in attention layer,
and the signal/noise vectors in the training data. The calculations characterize how the neural network at initialization randomly captures signal and noise information.

Lemma C.6
.

Suppose that
s=\Omega(\log(m_{v}n/\delta))
,
m_{v}=\Omega(\log(1/\delta))
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}^{2}d/2\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}^{(0)}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 3\sigma_{0}^{2}d/2,

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{2\log(12m_{v}/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert_{2},

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{\log(12m_{v}n/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s},

for all
r\in[m_{v}]
,
j\in\left\{\pm 1\right\}
and
i\in[n]
.

Lemma C.7
.

Suppose that
s=\Omega(\log(m_{v}n/\delta))
,
m_{v}=\Omega(\log(1/\delta))
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{\log(12m_{v}/\delta)}\cdot m_{v}^{-1/2}\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert_{2},

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{2\log(12m_{v}n/\delta)}\cdot m_{v}^{-1/2}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s},

for all
i\in[n]
.

Lemma C.8
.

Suppose that
s=\Omega(\log(m_{k}n/\delta))
,
m_{k}=\Omega(\log(1/\delta))
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}^{2}d/2\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 3\sigma_{0}^{2}d/2,

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert/2\leq\max_{s\in[m_{k}]}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{2\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert_{2},

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s}/4\leq\max_{s\in[m_{k}]}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s},

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}^{2}d/2\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 3\sigma_{0}^{2}d/2,

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert/2\leq\max_{s\in[m_{k}]}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{2\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert_{2},

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s}/4\leq\max_{s\in[m_{k}]}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq 2\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s},

for all
s\in[m_{k}]
, and
i\in[n]
.

Proof.

The proof of Lemma
C.6
,
C.7
and
C.8
are the same as the Lemma B.3 in
Cao et al. (2022)
.
∎

Define

\displaystyle\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}=\max_{i,s,j,r}\left\{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\right\},

(2)

\displaystyle\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}=\max_{s,j,r}\left\{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\right\}.

(3)

The following lemmas study the anti-concentration behaviour in a randomly initialized neural network.

Lemma C.9
.

Let
x\sim N(0,\sigma^{2})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\left|x\right|\leq c)\leq\erf\left(\frac{c}{\sqrt{2}\sigma}\right)\leq\sqrt{1-\exp(-\frac{2c^{2}}{\pi\sigma^{2}})}.

Proof.

The probability density function for
x
is given by

\displaystyle f(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}\exp(-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}).

Then we know that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|x|\leq c)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}\int_{-c}^{c}\exp(-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}})dx.

By the definition of
\mathrm{erf}
function

\displaystyle\mathrm{erf}(c)=\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}}\int_{0}^{c}\exp(-{x^{2}})dx,

and variable substitution yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{erf}(\frac{c}{\sqrt{2}\sigma})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}\int_{0}^{c}\exp(-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}})dx.

Therefore, we first conclude
\mathbb{P}(|x|\leq c)=2\mathrm{erf}(\frac{c}{\sqrt{2}\sigma})
.

Next, by the inequality
\mathrm{erf}(x)\leq\sqrt{1-\exp(-4x^{2}/\pi)}
, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|x|\leq c)\leq 2\sqrt{1-\exp(-\frac{2c^{2}}{\sigma^{2}\pi})}.

∎

Lemma C.10
.

Suppose the results in Lemma
C.8
and Lemma
C.4
hold.
Suppose that

\sigma_{p}^{2}s\geq\frac{32\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}{-\pi\log(1-\delta^{2}/4m_{k}^{2}n^{2})}
.
Then with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\geq 2\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}},\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\geq 2\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}},

for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]
.

Proof.

Given
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\sim N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2})
.
By Lemma
C.9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq c\right)\leq\sqrt{1-\exp(-\frac{2c^{2}}{\pi\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}})}.

Let
c=2\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}
.
By Lemma
C.8
,

\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}
can be upper bounded by
\sqrt{2\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert
.
By Lemma
C.4
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}^{2}s/2\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 3\sigma_{p}^{2}s/2,

for all
i\in[n]
. Therefore, if we set appropriately

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}^{2}s\geq\frac{32\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}{-\pi\log(1-\delta^{2}/4m_{k}^{2}n^{2})},

then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq 2\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{2m_{k}n}.

The analysis for
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
is exactly the same.
By union bound, we get the final result.
∎

Lemma C.11
.

Suppose the results in Lemma
C.8
and Lemma
C.4
hold.
Suppose that

m_{v}\geq\frac{256\log^{2}(12m_{k}n/\delta)}{\pi^{2}\log^{2}(1-\delta^{2}/4m_{k}^{2}n^{2})}
.
Then with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\geq\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}m_{v}^{-1/4},\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\geq\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}m_{v}^{-1/4},

for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]
.

Proof.

Given
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\sim N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2})
.
By Lemma
C.9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq c\right)\leq\sqrt{1-\exp(-\frac{2c^{2}}{\pi\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}})}.

Let
c=\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}m_{v}^{-1/4}
.
By Lemma
C.8
,

\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}
can be upper bounded by
2\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s}
.
By Lemma
C.4
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}^{2}s/2\leq\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 3\sigma_{p}^{2}s/2,

for all
i\in[n]
. Therefore, if we set appropriately

\displaystyle m_{v}\geq\frac{256\log^{2}(12m_{k}n/\delta)}{\pi^{2}\log^{2}(1-\delta^{2}/4m_{k}^{2}n^{2})},

then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}m_{v}^{-1/4}\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{2m_{k}n}.

The analysis for
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
is exactly the same.
By union bound, we get the final result.
∎

Lemma C.12
.

Suppose that

s\geq n^{3}\left(\frac{2}{-\pi\log(1-\delta^{2}/4m_{k}^{2})}\right)^{3/2}
.
Then with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\geq\sigma_{0}ns^{-1/3}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

for all
s\in[m_{k}]
.

Proof.

Note that
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\sim N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2})
.
By Lemma
C.9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq c\right)\leq\sqrt{1-\exp(-\frac{2c^{2}}{\pi\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2}})}.

Let
c=\sigma_{0}ns^{-1/3}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert
.
If we set appropriately

\displaystyle s\geq n^{3}\left(\frac{2}{-\pi\log(1-\delta^{2}/4m_{k}^{2})}\right)^{3/2},

then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq\sigma_{0}ns^{-1/3}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{2m_{k}}.

The analysis for
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
is exactly the same.
By union bound, we get the final result.
∎

The following lemma studies the magnitude of output of softmax operation.

Lemma C.13
.

Suppose that
s=\Omega(\log(m_{k}n/\delta))
,
m_{k}=\Omega(\log(1/\delta))
. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left|s_{i,11}^{(0)}-1/2\right|\leq 16m_{k}\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)\cdot\sigma_{0}^{2}\max\left\{\sigma_{p}^{2}s,\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}^{2}\right\rVert\right\},

\displaystyle\left|s_{i,21}^{(0)}-1/2\right|\leq 16m_{k}\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)\cdot\sigma_{0}^{2}\max\left\{\sigma_{p}^{2}s,\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}^{2}\right\rVert\right\},

for all
i\in[n]
. When
m_{k}\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)\cdot\sigma_{0}^{2}\max\left\{\sigma_{p}^{2}s,\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}^{2}\right\rVert\right\}=o(1)
, we can simplify the results into

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(0)},s_{i,21}^{(0)}=1/2+o(1),

for all
i\in[n]
.

Proof.

We directly have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{s=1}^{m_{k}}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{s=1}^{m_{k}}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{s=1}^{m_{k}}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\sum_{s=1}^{m_{k}}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}},

for all
i\in[n]
. Then we have

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}\right)}{\exp\left(m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}\right)+\exp\left(-m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(-m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}(\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}+\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 1/2+2m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}(\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}+\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}),

and

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\exp\left(-m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}\right)}{\exp\left(-m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}\right)+\exp\left(m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}(\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}+\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}})\right)}

\displaystyle\geq 1/2-2m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}(\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}+\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}),

for all
i\in[n]
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle 1/2-2m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}(\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}+\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}})\leq s_{i,11}^{(0)}\leq 1/2+2m_{k}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}(\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}+\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}})

for all
i\in[n]
. By Lemma
C.8
, with probability
1-\delta
,
\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}
can be bounded by
2\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s}
and
\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}
can be bounded by
\sqrt{2\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert
, and

\displaystyle\max\left\{\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}},\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}\right\}\leq 2\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)}\cdot\sigma_{0}\max\left\{\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s},\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert\right\},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.14
.

Suppose that
\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert=\Omega(d^{1/5})
.
Let

\displaystyle\penalty\ X_{i}:=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle,

\displaystyle\penalty\ Y_{i}:=-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle.

Then with probability at least
1-nd^{-1/5}
, for all
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)\geq-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle,

or

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\geq\frac{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle).

Proof.

We only need to show that for all
i\in[n]
the probability of following event can be controlled.

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}X_{i}<Y_{i}<\frac{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}X_{i}.

Conditioning on
y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}
, we have
X_{i}\,{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{d}}}\,Y_{i}\sim N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}))
,
this is because
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=0
. Denote

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sigma^{2}:=\sigma_{0}^{2}(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}),

\displaystyle\penalty\ D:=\left\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\frac{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}x<y<\frac{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}x\right\}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}X_{i}<Y_{i}<\frac{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}X_{i}|y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \iint_{D}1\cdot dP_{X_{i}}dP_{Y_{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \iint_{D}\frac{1}{2\pi\sigma^{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}+y^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)dxdy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \iint_{D}\frac{1}{2\pi\sigma^{2}}r\exp\left(-\frac{r^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)drd\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{\pi}{2}-2\arctan\left(\frac{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{4}{3}\frac{2\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ O(d^{-1/5}).

where the fifth step is by
\arctan(1+x)\geq\frac{\pi}{4}+\frac{2}{3}(x-1)
when
x=1-o(1)
,
and the last step is by the condition
\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert=\Omega(d^{1/5})
.
Then, by integrating over
y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}
and union bound, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\exists\penalty\ i\in[n],\penalty\ \frac{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}X_{i}<Y_{i}<\frac{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}X_{i}\right]\leq O(nd^{-1/5}).

∎

Next, let

\displaystyle S_{s,1,K+,Q-}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\left\{i\in[n]:\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0,\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle<0,y_{i}=1\right\},

(4)

\displaystyle S_{i,K+,Q-}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\left\{s\in[m_{k}]:\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0,\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle<0\right\},

(5)

\displaystyle S_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}},K+,Q-}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\left\{s\in[m_{k}]:\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle>0,\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle<0\right\},

(6)

\displaystyle S_{s,K+,Q-}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\left\{i\in[n]:\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0,\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle<0,\right\}=S_{s,1,K+,Q-}^{(0)}\cup S_{s,-1,K-,Q+}^{(0)}.

(7)

Appendix D
Gradient

In this section, we present the gradients of the loss
L_{S}
with respect to
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}
,
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}
for
s\in[m_{k}]
,
j\in\left\{\pm 1\right\}
and
r\in[m_{v}]
. Our analysis starts from a more general form:

\displaystyle F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}}):=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\textbf{1}^{\top}_{m_{v}}\sigma(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}}_{V,j}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}}\text{softmax}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}}_{K}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}}_{Q}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(l)}\right)),

and finally focus on
L=2
and
\sigma(x)=x
case, which is exactly the model defined in Eq. (
1
).

Lemma D.1

(softmax derivative)
.

Let
\left(s_{1},\dots,s_{n}\right)=\text{softmax}\left(z_{1},\dots,z_{n}\right)
, then we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial s_{i}}{\partial z_{j}}=s_{i}\left(\mathds{1}\left\{i=j\right\}-s_{j}\right).

Lemma D.2

(Gradient w.r.t. value parameters)
.

Consider the transformer model defined in Eq. (
1
).
Let
\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}},y\right)
be a data point. The derivative of
F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})
with respect to
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}
is:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}}F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\left[\left(s_{1,1}+s_{2,1}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+\left(s_{1,2}+s_{2,2}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right].

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}}F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{a=1}^{L}s_{l,a}\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(a)}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(a)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\left[\left(s_{1,1}+s_{2,1}\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+\left(s_{1,2}+s_{2,2}\right)\sigma^{\prime}\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\left[\left(s_{1,1}+s_{2,1}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+\left(s_{1,2}+s_{2,2}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right],

where the second step is by
L=2
, the third step is by
\sigma(x)=x
.
∎

Lemma D.3

(Gradient w.r.t. pre-softmax quantity)
.

Consider the transformer model defined in Eq. (
1
).
Let
\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}},y\right)
be a data point. The derivative of
F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})
with respect to
z_{l,a}
is:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})}{\partial z_{l,a}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}s_{l,1}s_{l,2}\left[\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}_{a}\right\rangle\right)-\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}_{3-a}\right\rangle\right)\right].

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}})}{\partial z_{l,a}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}\sum_{b=1}^{L}\frac{\partial s_{l,b}}{\partial z_{l,a}}\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(b)}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}\sum_{b\neq a}s_{l,a}s_{l,b}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(a)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(b)}\right\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}s_{l,a}\sum_{b\in[L]}s_{l,b}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(a)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(b)}\right\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}s_{l,1}s_{l,2}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}_{a}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}_{3-a}\right\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}s_{l,1}s_{l,2}\left[\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}_{a}\right\rangle\right)-\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}_{3-a}\right\rangle\right)\right],

where the penultimate step is by
L=2
,
the final step is by
\sigma(x)=x
.
∎

Lemma D.4

(Gradient w.r.t. query parameters)
.

Consider the transformer model defined in Eq. (
1
).
Let
\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}},y\right)
be a data point. The derivative of
F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})
with respect to
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}
is:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}}F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left(s_{1,1}s_{1,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+s_{2,1}s_{2,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}_{V,j},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left(s_{1,1}s_{1,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+s_{2,1}s_{2,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right).

Proof.

By Lemma
D.3
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}}F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,a}\frac{\partial F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})}{\partial z_{l,a}}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}}z_{l,a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,a}\left[\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{m\in[d_{v}]}s_{l,a}\sum_{b\in[L]}s_{l,b}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(a)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(b)}\right\rangle\right)\right]\right]\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(a)}\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,a}\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}s_{l,1}s_{l,2}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(a)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(3-a)}\right\rangle\right)\right]\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(a)}\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}s_{1,1}s_{1,2}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\right)\right]\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+s_{1,1}s_{1,2}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right)\right]\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}

\displaystyle+s_{2,1}s_{2,2}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\right)\right]\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}

\displaystyle+s_{2,1}s_{2,2}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right)\right]\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left(s_{1,1}s_{1,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+s_{2,1}s_{2,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left(s_{1,1}s_{1,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+s_{2,1}s_{2,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}_{V,j},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left(s_{1,1}s_{1,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+s_{2,1}s_{2,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right),

where the third step is by
L=2
, the last step is by
\sigma(x)=x
.
∎

Lemma D.5

(Gradient w.r.t. key parameters)
.

Consider the transformer model defined in Eq. (
1
).
Let
\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}},y\right)
be a data point. Similarly, The derivative of
F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})
with respect to
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}
is:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}}F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})

\displaystyle=\left\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}_{V,j},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s},s_{1,1}s_{1,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+s_{2,1}s_{2,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right).

Proof.

By Lemma
D.3
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}}F_{j}(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{W}},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{X}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,a}\left[\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}s_{l,a}\sum_{b\in[L]}s_{l,b}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(a)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(b)}\right\rangle\right)\right]\right]\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(l)}\right\rangle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(a)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}}\sum_{r\in[m_{v}]}\left[\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}\right\rangle\right)-\sigma\left(\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s},s_{1,1}s_{1,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+s_{2,1}s_{2,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}_{V,j},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s},s_{1,1}s_{1,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}+s_{2,1}s_{2,2}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right\rangle\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{x}}^{(2)}\right),

where the second step is by
L=2
, the third step is by
\sigma(x)=x
.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

Consider the transformer model defined in Eq. (
1
).
Let
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{V,j,r}
for
j\in\left\{\pm 1\right\},\penalty\ r\in[m_{v}]
be the value parameters of the TF at the
t
-th iteration. If we use sign gradient descent, the update formula of
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{V,j,r}
is

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{V,j,r}=\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{V,j,r}-\eta\sgn\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}y_{i}j\left[(s_{i,11}^{(t)}+s_{i,21}^{(t)})y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}+(s_{i,12}^{(t)}+s_{i,22}^{(t)})\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right]\right).

Proof.

This is by Lemma
D.2
.
∎

Lemma D.7
.

Consider the transformer model defined in Eq. (
1
).
Let
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s}
for
s\in[m_{k}]
be the query and key parameters of the TF at the
t
-th iteration.
If we use sign gradient descent, the update formula of
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s}
is

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s}=\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s}-\eta\sgn\left[\sum_{i\in[n]}{\ell}^{\prime(t)}_{i}y_{i}\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,1}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,-1},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle(s_{11}^{(t)}s_{12}^{(t)}y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}+s_{21}^{(t)}s_{22}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i})\right].

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s}=\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s}-\eta\sgn\left[\sum_{i\in[n]}{\ell}^{\prime(t)}_{i}y_{i}\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,1}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,-1},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},s_{11}^{(t)}s_{12}^{(t)}y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}+s_{21}^{(t)}s_{22}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle(y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i})\right].

Proof.

This is by Lemma
D.4
, Lemma
D.5
.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs

In this section, we give a detailed analysis about the dynamics of sign gradient descent on our transformer models Eq. (
1
) with our data model in Def.
2.1
.
These results are based on Condition
3.1
and the conclusions in Appendix
C
, which hold with high probability.
Denote by
\mathcal{E}_{\text{prelim}}
the event that all the results in Appendix
C
hold (for a given
\delta
, we see
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\text{prelim}})\geq 1-10\delta-n^{-2}-nd^{-1/5}
by a union bound).
For simplicity and clarity, we state all the results in this and the following sections conditional on
\mathcal{E}_{\text{prelim}}
.

E.1
Technique Overview

For value, the dynamics is linear under sign gradient descent, which means the increment is exactly constant across different iterations and different neurons, and up to a constant multiplier across different samples.

For query and key, the dynamics is complicated. The key point is that we utilize the different increasing speed of different core quantities to divide the whole timeline into many stages where we can care about only one of or some of quantities in each stage while the dynamics of other quantities can be ignored or is very simple.
The main reason why the speed is different is attributed to the scale of hyperparameters including
\sigma_{p}s,\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s},\sigma_{0}
.

We will define some key timesteps in following analysis, including

\displaystyle T_{1}:=\tilde{\Theta}(\sigma_{0}\eta^{-1}s^{-1/2}m_{v}^{-1/2}),

\displaystyle T_{2}:=50\sqrt{2}n\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}\eta^{-1}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}=\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}s^{-1/2}\eta^{-1}),

\displaystyle T_{2}^{+}:=C_{1}\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)}\sigma_{0}ns^{-1/2}\eta^{-1}=\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}s^{-1/2}\eta^{-1}),

\displaystyle T_{3}:=3\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}\eta^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{-1}=\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}\eta^{-1}),

\displaystyle T_{3}^{+}:=C_{2}\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}\sigma_{0}\eta^{-1}=\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}\eta^{-1}),

\displaystyle T_{4}^{-}\geq\tilde{T}_{4}^{-}:=\sqrt{\frac{0.99\pi}{2}}\sqrt{\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{p}s}{3\sqrt{2}n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\right)}\eta^{-1}m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}=\tilde{O}(m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\eta^{-1}),

\displaystyle T_{4}^{-}\leq\hat{T}_{4}^{-}:=\sqrt{\frac{1.01\pi}{2}}\sqrt{\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{p}s}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\right)}\eta^{-1}m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}=\tilde{O}(m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\eta^{-1}),

\displaystyle T_{4}^{+}\leq(1+\theta_{c})T_{2}^{-},

\displaystyle T_{4}:=C_{3}\log\left(\frac{C_{3}\sigma_{p}s}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\right)\eta^{-1}m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}=\tilde{O}(m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\eta^{-1}),

where
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}=\Theta(1)
are large constants. The order among these timesteps is

\displaystyle T_{1}\ll T_{2}<T_{2}^{+}\ll T_{3}<T_{3}^{+}\ll T_{4}^{-}<T_{4}^{+}<T_{4}.

Our analysis basically consists of following parts:

•

Mean value noise.
Let

\displaystyle T_{1}:=\tilde{\Theta}(\sigma_{0}\eta^{-1}s^{-1/2}m_{v}^{-1/2}).

For
t\in[0,T_{1}]
, the mean value across all samples align quickly while other inner product barely move.
For
t\geq T_{1}
, we have the mean value noise is linear with time

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}t\eta\sigma_{p}s\leq\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\leq 2t\eta\sigma_{p}s,

for all
i\in[n]
, and mean value signal
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
can be ignored compared with mean value noise
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
.
Moreover, for
t\geq T_{2}
, we show the mean value noise across different samples are almost the same

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}t\eta\sigma_{p}s(1+\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2})),

for all
i\in[n]
.

•

Useful bounds.

For
t\in[0,T_{3}^{+}]
, noise-signal softmax outputs are close to initialization, i.e., for all
i\in[n]

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(t)}=1/2+o(1).

For
t\in[0,T_{4}]
, signal-signal softmax outputs are close to initialization, i.e., for all
i\in[n]

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(t)}=1/2+o(1).

For
t\in[0,T_{2}^{+}]
, query/key signal are close to initialization, i.e., for all
s\in[m_{k}]

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|=\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|(1+o(1)),

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|=\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|(1+o(1)).

For
t\in[0,T_{4}]
, the loss derivative are close to initialization, i.e., for all
i\in[n]

\displaystyle\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}=1/2+o(1).

For
t\in[0,T_{4}^{-}]
, the ratio of noise-signal softmax outputs are close to 1, i.e., for all
i,k\in[n]

\displaystyle\frac{s_{i,21}^{(t)}}{s_{k,21}^{(t)}}=1+o(1).

•

Query/Key noise dynamics part i.
Let

\displaystyle T_{2}^{+}:=C_{1}\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)}\sigma_{0}ns^{-1/2}\eta^{-1},

\displaystyle T_{2}:=50\sqrt{2}n\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}\eta^{-1}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}=\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}s^{-1/2}\eta^{-1}).

where
C_{1}=\Theta(1)
is a large constant.
For
t\in[0,T_{2}]
, we focus on the dynamics of query/key noise. As mentioned above, softmax outputs and query/key signal are stuck at initialization,
so we can ignore the variation of query/key signal and softmax outputs before
T_{2}
.
All query/key noise can be divided into two groups. Given
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]
,

1.

if
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
, then they encourage each other to increase (in magnitude).

2.

if
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
, then they first encourage each other to decrease in magnitude, but rebound quickly and reduce to the first case.

For
t\in[T_{2},T_{2}^{+}]
, we have some nice properties about query/key noise

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)}y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle t\eta\sigma_{p}s/2\leq\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq 2t\eta\sigma_{p}s,

for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]
.
Also we have some nice properties about the sum of query/key noise
\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
,
\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\geq 2n\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}},

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\eta\sigma_{p}s,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\eta\sigma_{p}s,

for all
s\in[m_{k}]
.

•

Query/Key signal dynamics part i.

Let

\displaystyle T_{3}:=3\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}\eta^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{-1}=\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}\eta^{-1}),

\displaystyle T_{3}^{+}:=C_{2}\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}\sigma_{0}\eta^{-1},

where
C_{2}=\Theta(1)
is a large constant.
For
t\in[T_{2},T_{3}]
, we focus on the dynamics of query/key signal. In this period, softmax outputs are stuck at initialization, and
the dynamics of query/key noise keep unchanged

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)}y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\eta\sigma_{p}s,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\eta\sigma_{p}s,

for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]
.
We show that the update direction of query/key noise is determined by the summation of query/key noise.
For
t\in[T_{2},T_{3}]
, for all
s\in[m_{k}]
we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\sgn(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-\sgn(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert.

For
t\in[T_{3},T_{3}^{+}]
, we have some nice properties about query/key signal

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=_{\sgn}-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=_{\sgn}\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

\displaystyle t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert/2\leq\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq 2t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

for all
s\in[m_{k}]
.

•

Query/Key signal and noise dynamics part ii.

Let
T_{4}^{-}\geq T_{3}
be the first time the following condition does not hold, for all
s\in[m_{k}]

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\geq\frac{1}{2}n\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|,

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\geq 2s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|.

Let
T_{4}^{+}\geq T_{4}^{-}
be the first time the following condition holds, for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|.

Let

\displaystyle T_{4}=C_{3}\log\left(\frac{C_{3}\sigma_{p}s}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\right)\eta^{-1}m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1},

where
C_{3}=\Theta(1)
is a large constant
We discuss the dynamics by three parts.
For
t\in[T_{3},T_{4}^{-}]
, see stage IV.a in Appendix
E.7.1
.
For
t\in[T_{4}^{-},T_{4}^{+}]
, see stage IV.b in Appendix
E.7.2
.
For
t\in[T_{4}^{+},T_{4}]
, see stage IV.c in Appendix
E.7.1
.
For
t\geq T_{4}
, we have for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\sgn(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{3})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-\sgn(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{3})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)}y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=_{\sgn}-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle.

Note that we have following bounds for
T_{4}^{-}
and
T_{4}^{+}

\displaystyle T_{4}^{-}\geq\tilde{T}_{4}^{-}:=\sqrt{\frac{0.99\pi}{2}}\sqrt{\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{p}s}{3\sqrt{2}n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\right)}\eta^{-1}m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1},

\displaystyle T_{4}^{-}\leq\hat{T}_{4}^{-}:=\sqrt{\frac{1.01\pi}{2}}\sqrt{\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{p}s}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\right)}\eta^{-1}m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1},

\displaystyle T_{4}^{+}\leq(1+\theta_{c})T_{4}^{-}.

E.2
The dynamics of value

Write down the inner product between gradient of value parameters and signal & noise, we have

\displaystyle\langle\sgn\left(\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}}L\right),\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\sgn\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}l_{i}^{\prime(t)}j(s_{i,11}^{(t)}+s_{i,21}^{(t)})\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}[1]\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}[1]=-\sgn(j)\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

for all
j\in\left\{\pm 1\right\},r\in[m_{v}]
and

\displaystyle\langle\sgn\left(\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}}L\right),\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[d]}\sgn\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]}l_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime(t)}y_{i^{\prime}}j(s_{i^{\prime},12}^{(t)}+s_{i^{\prime},22}^{(t)})\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i^{\prime}}[k]\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k],

for all
i\in[n],j\in\left\{\pm 1\right\},r\in[m_{v}]
, and simplified to sparse setting

\displaystyle\langle\sgn\left(\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}}L\right),\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\sgn\left(l_{i}^{\prime(t)}y_{i}j(s_{i,12}^{(t)}+s_{i,22}^{(t)})\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k],

for all
i\in[n],j\in\left\{\pm 1\right\},r\in[m_{v}]
.

Consider the sparse property of the data, we have

\displaystyle\langle\sgn\left(\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,j,r}}L\right),\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\sgn\left(l_{i}^{\prime(t)}y_{i}j(s_{i,12}^{(t)}+s_{i,22}^{(t)})\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]

\displaystyle=-\sgn(y_{i}j)\sum_{k\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\left|\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]\right|

\displaystyle=-\sgn(y_{i}j)\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

where the last equality is due to
l^{\prime}<0
and
s_{i,ab}>0
for all
i\in[n],a,b\in[2]
.
Note the update to value parameters of is irrelevant to the magnitude of query, key, and attention weights. Then, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,y_{i},r}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,y_{i},r}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,-y_{i},r}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,-y_{i},r}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,y_{i},r}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,y_{i},r}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,-y_{i},r}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,-y_{i},r}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}.

As
t
become large, both
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,y_{i},r}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,y_{i},r}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
increase.
Also,
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{V,y_{i},r}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
increases faster since we have
s\sigma_{p}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{-1}=\tilde{\Omega}(1)
.

E.3
The dynamics of query and key: preparations

Additional Notations.

Let
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)}:=\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,1}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,-1}
.
When we talk about query and key parameters, we refer to
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}
for all
s\in[m_{k}]
.
When we talk about query/key signal (inner products), we refer to

\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
for all
s\in[m_{k}]
.
When we talk about query/key noise (inner products), we refer to

\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]
.
When we talk about (mean) value (noise), we refer to
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
for all
i\in[n]
.
We use
a=_{\sgn}b
to denote
\sgn(a)=\sgn(b)
.
Let
\mathcal{B}_{i}
be the support of sample
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}
in the training dataset.

Some Facts.

Based on gradient of query and key parameters and the sparse property of data, we firstly write down the inner product between gradient and signal & noise

\displaystyle\langle\sgn\left(\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}}L\right),\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sgn\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}{l}^{\prime(t)}_{i}y_{i}\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,1}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,-1},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}y_{i}\right),

\displaystyle\langle\sgn\left(\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}}L\right),\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\sgn\left({l}^{\prime(t)}_{i}y_{i}\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,1}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,-1},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k]\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k],

\displaystyle\langle\sgn\left(\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}}L\right),\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sgn\left(\sum_{i\in[n]}{l}^{\prime(t)}_{i}y_{i}\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,1}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,-1},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}+s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle y_{i}\right),

\displaystyle\langle\sgn\left(\nabla_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}}L\right),\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{B}_{i}}\sgn\left({l}^{\prime(t)}_{i}y_{i}\langle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,1}-\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}}^{(t)}_{V,-1},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}+s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle(-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k])\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}[k].

Before the formal analysis for the query/key dynamics, we have some observations to simplify the gradient update formula.
Firstly, we note that the update magnitude of query/key inner product is constant at all iterations. Formally, for all
t\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|=\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

(8)

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|=\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

(9)

which means that we only need to analyze the sign of update direction for all quantities of interest.

Secondly, we take a look at the key part in gradient formula for different quantities:

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-y_{i}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-y_{i}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}+\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+y_{i}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-y_{i}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle.

By the subsequent analysis in the stage I, we have that
the
\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|=o(1)\cdot\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}\rangle
such that we can approximately neglect the effect of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
.
Also, we have
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}\rangle\geq 0
.
Then, combined with the gradient formula, we have

•

For
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
, the sign of increment is aligned with

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle.

(10)

•

For
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
, the sign of increment is aligned with

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}(-l_{i}^{\prime(t)}s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)})\cdot\left(-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right).

(11)

•

For
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
, the sign of increment is aligned with

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle-\left(-s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle.

(12)

•

For
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
, the sign of increment is aligned with

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}(-l_{i}^{\prime(t)})\cdot\left(-s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right).

(13)

E.4
Stage I

In this part, we consider the dynamics of mean value signal and noise, i.e.
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
.
Roughly, at the end of this stage we want to show that mean value noise evolve fast and can be viewed as a linear variable with time, mean value signal is small and negligible afterwards, while query/key noise are very close to initialization.

From the analysis for value above, we directly have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+2\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

(14)

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+2\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}.

(15)

Let

\displaystyle T_{1}:=4\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}m_{v}^{-1/2}\eta^{-1}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}.

Note that we have
\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{2}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}m_{v}^{-1/2}
, then we have

\displaystyle t\eta\sigma_{p}s\leq\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\leq 2t\eta\sigma_{p}s,

for all
t\geq T_{1}
and
i\in[n]
.
However, the query/key noise do not deviate too much from the initialization at
T_{1}
since the deviation is at most

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{1})},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|}\leq 1+\frac{T_{1}\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}\right\rVert_{1}}{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|}\leq 1+\frac{O(\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}m_{v}^{-1/2})}{\Omega(\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}m_{v}^{-1/4})}=1+o(1),

where the second step is by Lemma
C.11
and magnitude of
T_{1}
.
Besides, the deviation of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(T_{1})},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
from initialization is also small. Formally, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(T_{1})},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|}\leq 1+\frac{T_{1}\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|}\leq 1+\frac{\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert{s}^{-1/2}{m_{v}}^{-1/2})}{\Omega(\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert ns^{-1/3}m_{v}^{-1/2})}=1+o(1),

where the second step is by Lemma
C.12

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=o(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle),

for all
i\in[n]
and
t\geq T_{1}
, where this holds at
t=T_{1}
due to Lemma
C.8
and the definition of
T_{1}
.
Combined with the form of gradient of query/key parameters, this makes us able to ignore the effect of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
afterwards.

E.5
Stage II

In this part, we consider the dynamics of all query/key noise. Roughly, at the end of this stage we want to show for all neurons
s\in[m_{k}]
and samples
i\in[n]
, query/key noise have the same sign and linear with
t
.

Let

\displaystyle T_{2}^{+}:=C_{1}\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}n/\delta)}\sigma_{0}ns^{-1/2}\eta^{-1},

where
C_{1}=\Theta(1)
is a large constant. In this section, we will analyze the dynamics of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
for all
s\in[m_{k}]
,
i\in[n]
and
t\leq T_{2}^{+}
.

We first consider the samples
i\in S_{s,K+,Q-}^{(0)}\cup S_{s,K-,Q+}^{(0)}
at initialization.
Note that without loss of generality, we can assume that
i\in S_{s,K-,Q+}^{(0)}
.
Then, by Lemma
C.14
, we only need to consider two cases where the one is when we have

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\leq\frac{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle),

and the other one is when we have

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\geq\frac{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle).

Finally, we give an upper bound for
T_{2,s,i}^{3}
(defined later) for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in S_{s,K+,Q-}^{(0)}\cup S_{s,K-,Q+}^{(0)}
, and bound the magnitude of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
before
T_{2}^{+}
.

We define following useful timesteps.
Let
T_{2,s,i}^{1}
be the first time satisfying

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle.

(16)

Let
T_{2,s,i}^{2}
be the first time satisfying

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)}y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle.

(17)

Let
T_{2,s,i}^{3}
be the first time satisfying Eq. (
16
), (
17
) and

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)}y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle.

(18)

Let
T_{2}^{\prime}:=\sqrt{2}\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}\eta^{-1}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}
.

The following lemma studies the order between
T_{2,s,i}^{1}
,
T_{2,s,i}^{2}
,
T_{2,s,i}^{3}
.

Lemma E.1
.

For all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in S_{s,K+,Q-}^{(0)}\cup S_{s,K-,Q+}^{(0)}
, we have

\displaystyle 0<T_{2,s,i}^{1}\leq T_{2,s,i}^{3},

\displaystyle 0<T_{2,s,i}^{2}\leq T_{2,s,i}^{3}.

Proof of Lemma
E.1
.

WLOG, we assume that
i\in S_{s,K-,Q+}^{(0)}
. By the definition of
S_{s,K-,Q+}^{(0)}
, we have

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}1.

Then, by the definition of
T_{2,s,i}^{1}
, we have
0<T_{2,s,i}^{1}
.

By the definition of
T_{2,s,i}^{3}
, we naturally have
T_{2,s,i}^{1}<T_{2,s,i}^{3}
and
T_{2,s,i}^{2}<T_{2,s,i}^{3}
.

At
t=0
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

where the first step is by Eq. (
10
)
and the second step is by Lemma
C.10
,
and

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

where the first step is by Eq. (
12
)
and the second step is by Lemma
C.10
and
C.13
,
which implies
0<T_{2,s,i}^{2}
.

∎

Note that by Lemma
E.14
, we have that for all
i\in[n]
and
t\leq T_{2}^{+}
,

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(t)},s_{i,21}^{(t)}=s_{i,11}^{(0)}(1+o(1)),s_{i,21}^{(0)}(1+o(1))=1/2+o(1).

In the following analysis, we will use this fact many times.

When
-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\leq\frac{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)
, the update direction are determined by query noise.

By the definition of
T_{2,s,i}^{2}
, we must have that

\displaystyle(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2}+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2}+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)>0.

Note that we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2}+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\penalty\ -\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ -\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+T_{2,s,i}^{2}\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\geq 0,
