\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2}+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\penalty\ s_{i,11}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}s_{i,12}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+s_{i,21}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}s_{i,22}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{4}(1\pm o(1))(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-T_{2,s,i}^{2}\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert))\geq 0,

(19)

where the last steps in both lines are by the condition

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\leq\frac{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle).

We claim that the sign of update would not change for
t\in[T_{2,s,i}^{2},T_{2}^{+}]
. Suppose at
t\leq\tilde{t}\in[T_{2,s,i}^{2},T_{2}^{+}]
, the induction hypothesis holds, then we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

which imply

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq 0,

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}s_{i,12}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+s_{i,21}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}s_{i,22}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq\frac{1}{4}(1\pm o(1))(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\geq 0,

then the conclusion holds and thus
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{3})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{3})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
.

Moreover, we have an upper bound for
T_{2,s,i}^{2}
that

\displaystyle T_{2,s,i}^{2}\leq\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle}{\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)}\leq\frac{1.5\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle}{\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}}\leq 1.5T_{2}^{\prime},

where the first step is by Eq. (
19
),
the second step is by Lemma
C.10
,
the last step is by the definition of
T_{2}^{\prime}
and Lemma
C.4
.

When
-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\geq\frac{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)
, the update direction are determined by key noise.

At
t=T_{2,s,i}^{2}
we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2}+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\penalty\ -\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ -\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+T_{2,s,i}^{2}\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\leq 0,

(20)

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2}+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\penalty\ s_{i,11}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}s_{i,12}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+s_{i,21}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}s_{i,22}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{4}(1\pm o(1))(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-T_{2,s,i}^{2}\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert))\leq 0,

where the last steps in both lines are by the condition

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\geq\frac{\sigma_{p}s+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle).

Then, similar to previous analysis, we have for
t\in[T_{2,s,i}^{2},T_{2}^{+}]
, we have

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle<0,

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle<0,

which implies
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{3})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{3})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle<0
,
and

\displaystyle T_{2,s,i}^{2}\leq\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle}{\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}+\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)}\leq\frac{1.5\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle}{\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}}\leq 1.5T_{2}^{\prime},

where the first step is by Eq. (
20
),
the second step is by Lemma
C.10
,
the last step is by the definition of
T_{2}^{\prime}
and Lemma
C.4
.

Next, we are ready to bound
T_{2,s,i}^{3}
.
Note that during
[0,T_{2,s,i}^{3}]
, only one of query and key noise change its update direction.
WLOG, assume query noise changes the update direction while key noise doesn’t.
For the key noise, we have with at most
T_{2}^{\prime}
steps, its sign can align with the sign of its update.
For the query noise, we possibly have that the sign at
T_{2,s,i}^{2}
can be contrary with the sign at initialization, but
with at most another
T_{2,s,i}^{2}
steps, its sign can align with the sign of its update (and at initialization).
By these analyses, we have

\displaystyle T_{2,s,i}^{3}\leq\max\left\{T_{2}^{\prime},2T_{2,s,i}^{2}\right\}\leq 3T_{2}^{\prime},

for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in S_{s,K+,Q-}^{(0)}\cup S_{s,K-,Q+}^{(0)}
.

In the main text, we define
T_{2}^{\text{SGN}}:=3T_{2}^{\prime}
.

The following lemma gives bounds for the magnitude of query/key noise before
T_{2}^{+}
.

Lemma E.2
.

For all
s\in[m_{k}]
,
i\in S_{s,K+,Q-}^{(0)}\cup S_{s,K-,Q+}^{(0)}
and
t\in[3T_{2}^{\prime},T_{2}^{+}]
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

and for all
t\in[12T_{2}^{\prime},T_{2}^{+}]

\displaystyle\penalty\ t\eta\sigma_{p}s/2\leq\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq 2t\eta\sigma_{p}s.

Proof.

The first two lines are shown in the above discussion. For the last line,
for the lower bound, suppose
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2,s,i}^{3})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
,
we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (t-3T_{2}^{\prime})\eta\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(3T_{2}^{\prime})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (t-3T_{2}^{\prime})\eta\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ t\eta\sigma_{p}s/2,

where the first step is by Lemma
C.4
,
the second step is by
3T_{2}^{\prime}\geq T_{2,s,i}^{3}
,
the third step is by
t\geq 12T_{2}^{\prime}
.
For the upper bound, we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|+t\eta\sigma_{p}s\leq 2t\eta\sigma_{p}s,

where the second step is by
t\geq T_{2}^{\prime}
.
∎

We next consider the samples
i\in S_{s,K+,Q+}^{(0)}\cup S_{s,K-,Q-}^{(0)}
at initialization.
The following lemma studies the update direction and magnitude of these neurons.

Lemma E.3
.

For all
s\in[m_{k}]
,
i\in S_{s,K+,Q+}^{(0)}\cup S_{s,K-,Q-}^{(0)}
and
t\in[0,T_{2}^{+}]
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

and for all
t\in[T_{2}^{\prime},T_{2}^{+}]

\displaystyle\penalty\ t\eta\sigma_{p}s/2\leq\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq 2t\eta\sigma_{p}s.

Proof.

The first two lines are shown in the above discussion.
For the last line, note that without loss of generality, we can assume that
i\in S_{s,K+,Q+}^{(0)}
.
We can show that the sign of update of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
are the same as their sign at initialization and would not change for
t\in[0,T_{2}^{+}]
. Formally

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}^{+}
.
Similar to the proof in Lemma
E.1
, the claim holds at
t=0
. Suppose at
t\leq t^{\prime}\in[0,T_{2}^{+}]
, the induction hypothesis holds, then we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\geq\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\geq\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

which imply

\displaystyle\penalty\ -\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ -\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\geq 0,

and

\displaystyle\penalty\ s_{i,11}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}s_{i,12}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+s_{i,21}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}s_{i,22}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime}+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{4}(1\pm o(1))\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle(1+o(1))+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t^{\prime})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert))\geq 0,

where the first step is by Lemma
E.14
.
Then we show the result for
t=t^{\prime}+1
and prove the claim.
Therefore, for the lower bound, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\geq t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\geq t\eta\sigma_{p}s/2,

where the first step and second step follow from the claim above,
the last step is by Lemma
C.4
,
and for the upper bound, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(0)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\leq T_{2}^{\prime}\eta\sigma_{p}s+t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\leq 2t\eta\sigma_{p}s,

where the first step is by the claim above,
the second step is by the definition of
T_{2}^{\prime}
,
the last step is by
t\geq T_{2}^{\prime}
and Lemma
C.4
.
∎

Remark E.4
.

Combining Lemma
E.2
and
E.3
, we have for all
t\in[12T_{2}^{\prime},T_{2}^{+}]
,
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]

\displaystyle t\eta\sigma_{p}s/2\leq\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq 2t\eta\sigma_{p}s.

(21)

Note that the upper bound also hold for all
t\geq T_{2}^{+}
since the magnitude of update of query/key noise is always
\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}
, i.e., Eq. (
9
) always holds.

Next, for given neuron
s
, we study the magnitude of the summation of query/key noise over all samples. The following lemma studies the dynamics and magnitude of query/key noise summation.

Lemma E.5
.

Let
T_{2}:=50nT_{2}^{\prime}
.
We have

1.

(update direction). Let
\epsilon_{s,i}:=\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(3T_{2}^{\prime})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)
.
We have the sign of the update of the summation of query/key noise would keep unchanged for
[3T_{2}^{\prime},T_{2}^{+}]
.
Formally, for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
t\in[3T_{3}^{\prime},T_{3}^{+}]

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\eta\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{s,i}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}.

2.

(magnitude of update). With probability at least
1-O(m_{k}/\sqrt{n})
, for all
s\in[m_{k}]

\displaystyle\left|\eta\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{s,i}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\right|\geq\eta\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}.

3.

(magnitude of query/key noise summation). For all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
t\in[T_{2},T_{2}^{+}]

\displaystyle\sgn(\eta\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{s,i}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1})\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,\sgn(\eta\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{s,i}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1})\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq 2n\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}.

To prove this lemma, we need the following lemma which studies the property of
\epsilon_{s,i}
.

Lemma E.6
.

Let

\displaystyle E_{s,+}:=\left\{i\in[n]:\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0\right\},

\displaystyle E_{s,-}:=\left\{i\in[n]:\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle<0\right\}.

Let

\displaystyle A_{s,i}:=\left\{\omega\in\Omega:-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle<\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right\}.

Let

\displaystyle X_{s,i}:=

\displaystyle\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K+,Q+}]+\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q+},A_{s,i}]+\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K+,Q-},A_{s,i}]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q-}]-\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q+},A^{c}_{s,i}]-\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K+,Q-},A^{c}_{s,i}].

Then we have

1.

(The distribution of
X_{s,i}
). For all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle X_{s,i}=\begin{cases}1,\text{w.p. 1/2}\\
-1,\text{w.p. 1/2}\end{cases}.

2.

(The probability of equal set size).

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\left|E_{s,+}\right|-\left|E_{s,-}\right|=0]\leq O(n^{-1/2}).

3.

(The probability of lucky neurons).

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}>0,\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{s,i}>0]\geq\frac{1}{4}-O(n^{-1/2}).

Proof.

By the definition of
X_{s,i}
and
\epsilon_{s,i}
, we have following equivalent formulations

\displaystyle\left|E_{s,+}\right|=\left|\left\{i\in[n]:\epsilon_{s,i}>0\right\}\right|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K+,Q+}]+\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q+},A_{s,i}]+\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K+,Q-},A_{s,i}],

\displaystyle\left|E_{s,-}\right|=\left|\left\{i\in[n]:\epsilon_{s,i}<0\right\}\right|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q-}]+\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q+},A^{c}_{s,i}]+\mathds{1}[i\in S_{s,K+,Q-},A^{c}_{s,i}].

Let

Q_{s,i}:=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
,

K_{s,i}:=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{k,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
,

\mu_{s}:=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
.
Then we have
\mu_{s}\sim N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2})
, and
Q_{s,i},K_{s,i}|y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\sim N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2})
.
Denote the distribution of
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}
and
\mu
by
P_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}
and
P_{\mu}
, respectively.

Let
p(\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}):=\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}=1|\mu_{s}=\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}]
,

p_{\mu}:=\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}=1|\mu_{s}=\mu]
.

Let
\phi(\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}):=\mathbb{P}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q+},A_{s,i}|\mu_{s}=\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}]
,

\phi_{\mu}:=\mathbb{P}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q+},A_{s,i}|\mu_{s}=\mu]
.
By the symmetry of
S_{s,K-,Q+}
and
S_{s,K+,Q-}
, we have
\mathbb{P}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q+},A_{s,i}|\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}]=\mathbb{P}[i\in S_{s,K+,Q-},A_{s,i}|\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}]
, which gives

\displaystyle p(\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i})=\frac{1}{4}+2\phi(\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}),

\displaystyle p_{\mu}=\int p(\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i})dP_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}}=\int\frac{1}{4}+2\phi(\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i})dP_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}}=\frac{1}{4}+2\phi_{\mu}.

By the symmetry of the gaussian density, for any
\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\phi(\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i})

\displaystyle=\int_{q>0,k<0,q+k>\mu}\exp(-\frac{q^{2}+k^{2}}{2\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}})dqdk

\displaystyle=\int_{q>0,k<0,q+k<-\mu}\exp(-\frac{q^{2}+k^{2}}{2\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}})dqdk

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}-\int_{q>0,k<0,q+k>-\mu}\exp(-\frac{q^{2}+k^{2}}{2\sigma_{0}^{2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}})dqdk

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}-\phi(-\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}),

which implies
\phi(\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i})-\frac{1}{8}
is an odd function, and thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q+},A_{s,i}|\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}]=\int\phi(\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i})dP_{\mu}=\frac{1}{8},

thus
\mathbb{P}[i\in S_{s,K-,Q+},A_{s,i}]=\frac{1}{8}
and
\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}=1]=\frac{1}{2}
, which proves the first result.

Note that given
\mu
,
X_{s,i}
are independent for
i\in[n]
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\left|E_{s,+}\right|-\left|E_{s,-}\right|=0]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}[\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{s,i}=0]

\displaystyle=\int\binom{n}{n/2}p_{\mu}^{n/2}(1-p_{\mu})^{n/2}dP_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\int\binom{n}{n/2}\frac{1}{2^{n}}dP_{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\binom{n}{n/2}\frac{1}{2^{n}}\leq\frac{10}{\sqrt{n}},

which proves the second result.

Recall that

\displaystyle\penalty\ \phi(\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i})=1/4-\phi(-\mu,\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i})\penalty\ \forall\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow

\displaystyle\penalty\ \phi_{\mu}=1/4-\phi_{-\mu}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow

\displaystyle\penalty\ p_{\mu}=1-p_{-\mu}.

The quantity of interest can be written as

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underbrace{\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}>0,\sum_{j\neq i}X_{s,i}>0]}_{I_{1}}=\int\underbrace{\sum_{k=\lfloor(n-1)/2\rfloor+1}^{n-1}\binom{n}{k}p_{\mu}^{k}(1-p_{\mu})^{n-k}}_{p_{n-1,\mu}}\cdot p_{\mu}dP_{\mu},

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underbrace{\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}>0,\sum_{j\neq i}X_{s,i}<0]}_{I_{2}}=\int\underbrace{\sum_{k=\lfloor(n-1)/2\rfloor+1}^{n-1}\binom{n}{k}p_{\mu}^{n-k}(1-p_{\mu})^{k}}_{q_{n-1,\mu}}\cdot p_{\mu}dP_{\mu},

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underbrace{\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}<0,\sum_{j\neq i}X_{s,i}>0]}_{I_{3}}=\int\sum_{k=\lfloor(n-1)/2\rfloor+1}^{n-1}\binom{n}{k}p_{\mu}^{k}(1-p_{\mu})^{n-k}\cdot(1-p_{\mu})dP_{\mu},

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underbrace{\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}<0,\sum_{j\neq i}X_{s,i}<0]}_{I_{4}}=\int\sum_{k=\lfloor(n-1)/2\rfloor+1}^{n-1}\binom{n}{k}p_{\mu}^{n-k}(1-p_{\mu})^{k}\cdot(1-p_{\mu})dP_{\mu}.

By the symmetry of
P_{\mu}
and
p_{\mu}
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}>0,\sum_{j\neq i}X_{s,i}>0]=\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}<0,\sum_{j\neq i}X_{s,i}<0],

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}>0,\sum_{j\neq i}X_{s,i}<0]=\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}<0,\sum_{j\neq i}X_{s,i}>0].

Since

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{4}-I_{2}-I_{3}=\int(p_{\mu}-(1-p_{\mu}))(p_{n-1,\mu}-q_{n-1,\mu})dP_{\mu}\geq 0,

where the last step is by
p_{n-1,\mu}>q_{n-1,\mu}
when
p_{\mu}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X_{s,i}>0,\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{s,i}>0]\geq I_{1}\geq\frac{1}{4}(1-\mathbb{P}[\sum_{j\neq i}X_{s,i}=0])\geq\frac{1}{4}-O(n^{-1/2}),

where the second step is by
I_{1}=I_{4}\geq I_{2}=I_{3}
, the third step is by the second result above, which concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
E.5
.

By Lemma
E.2
and
E.3
,
we have the update direction of each element
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
is unchanged for
t\in[3T_{2}^{\prime},T_{2}^{+}]
, which implies the first result.

By Lemma
E.6
, with probability
1-O(m_{k}/\sqrt{n})
, we have for all
s\in[m_{k}]
,

\left|E_{s,+}\right|-\left|E_{s,-}\right|\neq 0
.
Below, suppose this fact holds, and all the results are based on this fact which hold with high probability.

WLOG, for fixed neuron
s
, suppose
\left|E_{s,+}\right|-\left|E_{s,-}\right|\geq 1
, then by Lemma
C.5
, we have

\displaystyle\eta\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{s,i}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}\geq\eta\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}.

(22)

Finally, we note that at
t=12T_{2}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(12T_{3}^{\prime})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(12T_{3}^{\prime})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq-24T_{3}^{\prime}n\eta\sigma_{p}s.

By the definition of
T_{2}
, we have
T_{2}\geq(12+24\sqrt{2}n)T_{2}^{\prime}+2n\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}\eta^{-1}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}
.
Then, for
t\in[T_{2},T_{2}^{+}]
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq 2n\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}.

(23)

Similarly, if
\left|E_{s,+}\right|-\left|E_{s,-}\right|\leq-1
for some other neuron
s
, then for
t\in[T_{2},T_{2}^{+}]
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\leq-2n\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}.

∎

E.6
Stage III

Let

\displaystyle T_{3}^{+}:=C_{2}\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}\sigma_{0}\eta^{-1},

where
C_{2}=\Theta(1)
is a large constant. In this section, we will analyze the dynamics of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
for all
s\in[m_{k}]
,
i\in[n]
and
T_{2}\leq t\leq T_{3}^{+}
.
Generally, we already have controlled the direction and magnitude of the quantity
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
and
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
at the timestep
T_{2}
, we can show the update direction of query/key noise is determined by these quantities.

For
t\leq T_{2}
, we want to show the change of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
is not much.
Actually, we can show that for
t\leq T_{2}^{+}
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
are close to initialization. Formally, we have the following lemma.

Lemma E.7
.

For all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
t\leq T_{2}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\cdot(1\pm o(1)),

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\cdot(1\pm o(1)).

Proof.

For key signal, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|}{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|+t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|}\leq 1+\frac{t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\Omega(\sigma_{0}ns^{-1/3}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)}=1+o(1),

where the first step is by Eq. (
8
),
the second step is by Lemma
C.12
,
and the last step is by the definition of
T_{2}^{+}
.
This also imply the sign of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
is the same as initialization for all
t\leq T_{2}^{+}
. The proof for query signal is similar.
∎

For
t\geq T_{2}
, we want to show the update direction of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
or
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
) would be determined by the sign of
\sum_{i}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
or
\sum_{i}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
, respectively.
To this end, we need to control the weighting term to make the weighted summation of query/key noise similar to the ordinary summation, which enables us to apply the previous analysis.
These weighting terms include: (1) The softmax outputs; (2) The loss derivative; (3) The magnitude of mean value noise
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
.
We want to show that these quantities are almost same across all samples, or even keep constant before
T_{4}^{+}
.

The following lemma studies the magnitude of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
after
T_{2}
.
This lemma also shows
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
are close between different samples.

Lemma E.8
.

For all
i\in[n]
and
t\geq T_{2}
,
we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=2\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}t\eta\sigma_{p}s(1+\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2})).

Proof.

We can show

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+2t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle=2t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}(1+o(1))

\displaystyle=2\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}t\eta\sigma_{p}s(1+\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2}))(1+o(1)).

where the second step is by
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=O(\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}}m_{v}^{-1/2})=o(T_{2}\eta\sigma_{p}s)
,
the third step is by Lemma
C.4
.
∎

By Lemma
E.14
, we have that softmax outputs are concentrated at
1/2
for all
i\in[n]
and
t\leq T_{3}^{+}
.
By Lemma
E.15
, we have that the derivative of loss of all samples are concentrated at
1/2
for all
i\in[n]
and
t\leq T_{3}^{+}
.

With these properties, we are ready to show that the query/key noise dynamics keep unchanged for
[T_{2},T_{3}^{+}]
,
and the query/key signal dynamics are dominated by the sign of the summation of query/key noise, i.e.,
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
.
The next two lemmas characterize the dynamics of query/key noise and query/key signal, respectively.

Lemma E.9
.

For all
s\in[m_{k}]
,
i\in[n]
and
T_{2}\leq t\leq T_{3}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1},

and for all
t\in[T_{2},T_{3}^{+}]

\displaystyle\penalty\ t\eta\sigma_{p}s/2\leq\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq 2t\eta\sigma_{p}s.

Proof.

WLOG, given neuron
s\in[m_{k}]
and sample
i\in[n]
, suppose
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
, we claim that
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
are increasing for
T_{2}\leq t\leq T_{3}^{+}
. At
t=T_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2}+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq\tilde{\Omega}(\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s}n)-\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)>0,

and

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(T_{2}+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(T_{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(T_{2})}s_{i,12}^{(T_{2})}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+s_{i,21}^{(T_{2})}s_{i,22}^{(T_{2})}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{4}+o(1))(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{4}+o(1))(\tilde{\Omega}(\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}\sqrt{s}n)-\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert))>0.

Suppose the induction holds for
t\leq\tilde{t}-1
, then at
t=\tilde{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(\tilde{t}+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(\tilde{t})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(\tilde{t})}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(\tilde{t})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{3})}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{3})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+(\tilde{t}-T_{3})\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}\right\rVert_{1}-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)>0,

and

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(\tilde{t}+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(\tilde{t})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(\tilde{t})}s_{i,12}^{(\tilde{t})}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(\tilde{t})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+s_{i,21}^{(\tilde{t})}s_{i,22}^{(\tilde{t})}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(\tilde{t})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{4}+o(1))(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(\tilde{t})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(\tilde{t})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{4}+o(1))\left(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{3})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{3})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+(\tilde{t}-T_{3})\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}\right\rVert_{1}-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\right)>0.

Finally, the bounds about magnitude follow from Lemma
E.2
,
E.3
and
C.4
.
∎

Remark E.10
.

Lemma
E.9
implies that the dynamics of
\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
,

\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
keep unchanged for
T_{2}\leq t\leq T_{3}^{+}
and
for all
s\in[m_{k}]
.

Lemma E.11
.

For all
s\in[m_{k}]
,
i\in[n]
and
T_{2}\leq t\leq T_{3}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\sgn(\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-\sgn(\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert.

Proof.

WLOG, given neuron
s\in[m_{k}]
, suppose we have
\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
,
thus
\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
.
Therefore, for
T_{2}\leq t\leq T_{3}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}(-l_{i}^{\prime(t)}s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)})\cdot\left(-y_{i}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{8\sqrt{\pi}}t\eta\sigma_{p}s(1+o(1))\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+(t-T_{2})(\eta\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}-\eta n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)>0,

where the first inequality is due to the lower bound Eq. (
22
),
and the second inequality is due to the lower bound Eq. (
23
) and
n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert=o(\sigma_{p}s)
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}(-l_{i}^{\prime(t)})\cdot\left(-y_{i}s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{8\sqrt{\pi}}t\eta\sigma_{p}s(1+o(1))\cdot(-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-(t-T_{2})(\eta\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}-\eta n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)<0.

∎

Lemma E.12
.

Let
T_{3}:=3\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}\eta^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{-1}=\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}\eta^{-1})
, then for
T_{3}\leq t\leq T_{3}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert/2\leq\sgn(\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\leq 4t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert/3,

(24)

\displaystyle-4t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert/3\leq\sgn(\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\cdot\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\leq-t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert/2.

(25)

Proof.

WLOG, given neuron
s\in[m_{k}]
, suppose we have
\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
.
We only prove Eq. (
24
). The proof of Eq. (
25
) is identical.
For the upper bound, for
t\in[T_{3},T_{3}^{+}]
we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\leq\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}+t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert\leq(t+T_{3}/3)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert\leq 4t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert/3,

where the first step is by Eq. (
8
),
the second step is by the definition of
T_{3}
.
For the lower bound, for
t\in[T_{3},T_{3}^{+}]
we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\geq-\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}-T_{2}\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert+(t-T_{2})\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert\geq(t-T_{3}/3-2T_{2})\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert\geq t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert/2,

where the first step is by Eq. (
8
),
the second step is by definition of
T_{3}
,
the third step is by
T_{2}T_{3}^{-1}=o(1)
.
∎

Remark E.13
.

Note that the upper bound in Eq. (
24
)
and lower bound in Eq. (
25
)
hold for all
t\geq T_{3}
since the magnitude of update of query/key signal is always
\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert
, i.e., Eq. (
8
) always holds.

E.7
Stage IV

This section study the concentration behavior of softmax outputs and loss derivative.
We first show the magnitude of softmax outputs at initialization
s_{i,11}^{(t)}
and
s_{i,21}^{(t)}
are concentrated at 1/2 for all
i\in[n]
before
T_{4}
and
T_{3}^{+}
, respectively.

Lemma E.14
.

Let

\displaystyle T_{4}:=C_{3}\log\left(\frac{C_{3}\sigma_{p}s}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\right)\eta^{-1}m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}

(26)

where
C_{3}=\Theta(1)
is a large constant. Then, for all
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(t)}=1/2\pm o(1),\penalty\ \forall t\leq T_{4},

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(t)}=1/2\pm o(1),\penalty\ \forall t\leq T_{3}^{+}.

Proof of Lemma
E.14
.

Note that for
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|+2t\eta\sigma_{p}s

\displaystyle\leq 4\max\left\{\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}},t\eta\sigma_{p}s\right\},

for all
i\in[n]
and
s\in[m_{k}]
and

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(0)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|+t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 4\max\left\{\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}},t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert\right\},

for all
s\in[m_{k}]
. Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4\max\left\{\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}},t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert\right\},

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4\max\left\{\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}},t\eta\sigma_{p}s\right\}.

When
t\leq T_{3}^{+}
, we have for all
i\in[n]

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq O(\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}s),

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq O(\sqrt{\log(12m_{k}/\delta)}\sigma_{0}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert).

Then we have for all
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right)}{\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right)+\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(O(m_{k}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{p}s\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\right)}{\exp\left(O(m_{k}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{p}s\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\right)+\exp\left(-O(m_{k}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2})\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(-O(m_{k}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)\sigma_{0}^{2}(\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2}+\sigma_{p}s\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert))\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}+O(m_{k}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)\sigma_{0}^{2}(\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2}+\sigma_{p}s\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)),

and

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right)}{\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right)+\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\exp\left(-O(m_{k}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{p}s\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\right)}{\exp\left(-O(m_{k}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{p}s\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\right)+\exp\left(O(m_{k}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)\sigma_{0}^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2})\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(O(m_{k}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)\sigma_{0}^{2}(\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2}+\sigma_{p}s\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert))\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-O(m_{k}\log(12m_{k}/\delta)\sigma_{0}^{2}(\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2}+\sigma_{p}s\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)).

Similarly, when
t\leq T_{4}
, we have for all
i\in[n]

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\leq O(\log(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)m_{k}^{-1/2}),

(27)

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|,\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|\leq O(\log(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert).

(28)

Then we have for all
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right)}{\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right)+\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(O(\log^{2}(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\sigma_{p}^{-2}s^{-2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2})\right)}{\exp\left(O(\log^{2}(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\sigma_{p}^{-2}s^{-2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2})\right)+\exp\left(-O(\log^{2}(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(-O(\log^{2}(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert(1+\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert))\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}+O(\log^{2}(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert(1+\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)),

and

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right)}{\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right)+\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\exp\left(-O(\log^{2}(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\sigma_{p}^{-2}s^{-2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2})\right)}{\exp\left(-O(\log^{2}(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\sigma_{p}^{-2}s^{-2}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert^{2})\right)+\exp\left(O(\log^{2}(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(O(\log^{2}(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert(1+\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert))\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}-O(\log^{2}(C_{3}\sigma_{p}s/\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert(1+\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)).

∎

The next lemma shows that loss derivative
\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}
are concentrated at 1/2 for all
i\in[n]
before
T_{4}
.

Lemma E.15
.

Recall the
T_{4}
defined in Eq. (
26
).
For all
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}=1/2\pm o(1),\penalty\ \forall t\leq T_{4}.

Proof of Lemma
E.15
.

Note that
\ell(z)=\log(1+\exp(-z))
and
-\ell^{\prime}(z)=1/(1+\exp(z))
. For
t\leq T_{3}^{+}
we have

\displaystyle\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\exp\left((s_{i,11}^{(t)}+s_{i,21}^{(t)})\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+(s_{i,12}^{(t)}+s_{i,22}^{(t)})\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(y_{i}(1\pm o(1))(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(y_{i}(1\pm o(1))\tilde{O}(\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}s)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\pm o(1),

where the first step is by definition,
the second step is by Lemma
E.14
,
the third step is by Lemma
E.8
,
the last step is due to
\sigma_{0}\sigma_{p}s=o(1)
.
For
t\leq T_{4}
, suppose
y_{i}=1
, we have
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle,\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
, then

\displaystyle\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\exp\left((s_{i,11}^{(t)}+s_{i,21}^{(t)})\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+(s_{i,12}^{(t)}+s_{i,22}^{(t)})\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}+o(1),

where the first step is by definition,
the second step is by
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle,\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
, and

\displaystyle\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\exp\left((s_{i,11}^{(t)}+s_{i,21}^{(t)})\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+(s_{i,12}^{(t)}+s_{i,22}^{(t)})\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(y_{i}(3/2\pm o(1))(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(y_{i}(3/2\pm o(1))\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\exp\left(y_{i}(3/2\pm o(1))\tilde{O}(m_{k}^{-1/2})\right)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}-o(1),

where the first step is by definition,
the second step is by Lemma
E.14
,
the third step is by Lemma
E.8
,
the fourth and last step is by
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(T_{4})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\tilde{O}(m_{k}^{-1/2})=o(1)
.
∎

E.7.1
Stage IV.a

This stage studies the dynamics in the interval
[T_{3},T_{4}^{-}]
, show the fast decay of post-softmax weights and their impact.
Roughly, everything keeps unchanged in this stage.
The idea is that everything keeps going on as usual before
T_{4}^{-}
, which is defined later.
And we don’t control the behaviour between the interval
[T_{4}^{-},T_{4}^{+}]
, since
T_{4}^{-}
is not too far from
T_{4}^{+}
, we still can keep the good property at
T_{4}^{+}
even in the worst case.
Finally, after
T_{4}^{+}
, the good behaviour continues.
(Also at
T_{4}^{+}
, we have the softmax ratio is basically concentrated at 1.)

Let
T_{4}^{-}\geq T_{3}
be the last time the such that for all
t\in[T_{3},T_{4}^{-}]
,
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]
, following conditions hold,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\geq\frac{1}{2}n\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|,

(29)

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\geq 2s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|.

(30)

Note that at
T_{3}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-(t-T_{2})\eta(\frac{\sigma_{p}s}{4\sqrt{2}}-\frac{n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{2})>0,

and

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-2s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}(1+o(1))\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}(1+o(1))\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}(1+o(1))\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}(1+o(1))\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-(t-T_{2})\eta(\frac{\sigma_{p}s}{4\sqrt{2}}-\frac{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{2})>0,

which implies
T_{4}^{-}
is well-defined.

We use induction on
t
to simultaneously prove the following properties for all
t=T_{3},\dots,T_{4}^{-}
:

•

\mathcal{A}(t)
, the linear-with-
t
estimation: for all
i\in[n]
and
s\in[m_{k}]

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|=t\eta\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s(1\pm\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2})),

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|=t\eta\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s(1\pm\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2})).

•

\mathcal{D}(t)
, query noise is increasing at
t
: for all
i\in[n]
and
s\in[m_{k}]

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}.

•

\mathcal{E}(t)
, key noise is increasing at
t
: for all
i\in[n]
and
s\in[m_{k}]

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}.

•

\mathcal{F}(t)
, query/key noise have the same sign at
t
: for all
i\in[n]
and
s\in[m_{k}]

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=_{\sgn}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle.

Claim E.16
.

\mathcal{F}(t)\Longrightarrow\mathcal{E}(t)
.

Claim E.17
.

\mathcal{D}(T_{3}),\mathcal{E}(T_{3}),\dots,\mathcal{E}(t-1),\mathcal{F}(T_{3}),\dots,\mathcal{F}(t-1)\Longrightarrow\mathcal{D}(t)
.

Claim E.18
.

\mathcal{A}(t),\mathcal{D}(t),\mathcal{E}(t)\Longrightarrow\mathcal{A}(t+1)
.

Claim E.19
.

\mathcal{F}(t),\mathcal{D}(t),\mathcal{E}(t)\Longrightarrow\mathcal{F}(t+1)
.

Note that we have
\mathcal{A}(T_{3})
,
\mathcal{D}(T_{3})
,
\mathcal{F}(T_{3})
hold.

\mathcal{D}(T_{3})
and
\mathcal{F}(T_{3})
are already shown in the stage III.

Proof of
\mathcal{A}(T_{3})
.

At
t=T_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=(t-T_{2})\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=t\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}(1\pm O(T_{2}t^{-1}))

\displaystyle=t\eta\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s(1\pm\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2}))(1\pm O(T_{2}t^{-1}))=t\eta\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s(1\pm\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2})),

where the first step follows from the monotonicity of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
for
[T_{2},T_{3}]
,
the second step follows from Eq. (
21
), the estimate of the magnitude of
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
,
the third step follows from Lemma
C.4
, the concentration of
\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}
,
the last step follows from
T_{2}/T_{3}=\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2})
. The analysis is similar for
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
.
∎

Proof of Claim
E.16
.

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle,

where the first step is by Eq. (
12
), the second step is by the definition of
T_{4}^{-}
, i.e., Eq. (
30
).
∎

Proof of Claim
E.17
.

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{3})}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{3})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle+(t-T_{3})\eta(\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}-\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)>0,

where the first step is by Eq. (
10
), the second step is due to

\mathcal{E}(T_{3}),\dots,\mathcal{E}(t-1),\mathcal{F}(T_{3}),\dots,\mathcal{F}(t-1)
, the third step is by
\mathcal{D}(T_{3})
.
∎

Proof of Claim
E.18
.

\displaystyle\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|+\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\right\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle=t\eta\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s(1\pm\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2}))+\eta\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s(1\pm\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2}))

\displaystyle=(t+1)\eta\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma_{p}s(1\pm\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2})),

where the first step is by
\mathcal{D}(t)
, the second step is by
\mathcal{A}(t)
and Lemma
C.4
. The proof is same for
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
.
∎

Proof of Claim
E.19
.

Suppose
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
, then by
\mathcal{D}(t)
we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert>0.

By
\mathcal{F}(t)
, we have
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
, then by
\mathcal{E}(t)
we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert>0,

which gives
\mathcal{F}(t+1)
.
∎

Now we study the ratio of softmax outputs across different samples. The following lemma is crucial to prove the lower bound of
T_{4}^{-}
.

Lemma E.20
.

Let

\displaystyle t^{*}:=\min\left\{T_{4}^{-},T_{4}\right\}.

(31)

Then, for all
T_{3}\leq t\leq t^{*}
and all
i,k\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{s_{i,21}^{(t)}}{s_{k,21}^{(t)}}=1\pm o(1),\penalty\ \forall i,k\in[n].

Proof of Lemma
E.20
.

By
\mathcal{A}(t)
and
\mathcal{F}(t)
we have for
t\in[T_{3},T_{4}^{-}]
and all
i\in[n]

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle=\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}t^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2}(1\pm\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2})).

(32)

Then, for all
T_{3}\leq t\leq t^{*}
and
i\in[n]

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right)}{\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right)+\exp\left(\sum_{s\in[m_{k}]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1\pm o(1)+\exp\left(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}t^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2}(1\pm\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2}))\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1\pm o(1)+\exp(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}t^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2})\exp(\pm\tilde{O}(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}t^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{3/2}))}

\displaystyle=\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1\pm o(1)+\exp(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}t^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2})(1\pm o(1))}

\displaystyle=\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1+\exp(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}t^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2})},

(33)

where the first step is by definition, the second step is by Eq. (
28
), (
27
), and Eq. (
32
), the fourth step is by
m_{k}T_{4}^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2}=o(1)
. Then we have for all
T_{3}\leq t\leq t^{*}
and all
i,k\in[n]

\displaystyle\frac{s_{i,21}^{(t)}}{s_{k,21}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle=(1\pm o(1))\frac{(1\pm o(1))(1+\exp(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}t^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2}))}{(1\pm o(1))(1+\exp(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}t^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2}))}=1\pm o(1),

where the first step is by Eq. (
33
).
∎

The following lemma studies the lower bound for
T_{4}^{-}
.

Lemma E.21
.

We have

\displaystyle T_{4}^{-}\geq\tilde{T}_{4}^{-}:=\sqrt{\frac{0.99\pi}{2}}\sqrt{\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{p}s}{3\sqrt{2}n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\right)}\eta^{-1}m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}.

(34)

Proof.

Suppose
T_{4}^{-}<\tilde{T}_{4}^{-}
, thus we have
T_{4}^{-}+1<\tilde{T}_{4}^{-}+1<T_{4}
.
By Claim
E.18
and
E.19
, we have
\mathcal{A}(T_{4}^{-}+1)
and
\mathcal{F}(T_{4}^{-}+1)
hold, which implies Eq. (
32
) holds at
t=T_{4}^{-}+1
. Then, for all
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1\pm o(1)+\exp\left(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}(T_{4}^{-}+1)^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2}(1\pm\tilde{O}(s^{-1/2}))\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1+\exp(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}(T_{4}^{-}+1)^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2})}

(35)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1+\exp(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}(\tilde{T}_{4}^{-}+1)^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2})}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1.01\exp(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}(\tilde{T}_{4}^{-}+1)^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2})}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1.01}\left(\frac{3\sqrt{2}n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{\sigma_{p}s}\right)^{0.99+o(1)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(1\pm o(1))3\sqrt{2}n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{1.01\sigma_{p}s},

(36)

where the first step is by Eq. (
28
), (
27
), and Eq. (
32
),
the second step is by
T_{4}^{-}+1<T_{4}
,
the fifth step is by
\eta=o(m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1})
,
the last step is by
n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}=o(1)
,
Then we estimate the magnitude of
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
.
WLOG, given neuron
s
, suppose
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{3})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle>0
.
For
T_{3}<t\leq T_{4}^{-}+1

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(t-T_{2})\eta\sigma_{p}s+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(t-T_{2})\eta\sigma_{p}s+2n\beta_{\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}t\eta\sigma_{p}s(1\pm O(T_{2}t^{-1}))=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}t\eta\sigma_{p}s(1\pm o(1)),

(37)

where the first step follows from
\mathcal{D}(t)
and Eq. (
22
),
the second step follows from the lower bound of
\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{2})},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
Eq. (
23
),
the last step follows from
T_{2}T_{3}^{-1}=o(1)
.
The analysis is similar for
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
.
Now we can show that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i,21}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}s_{i,22}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1+\exp(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}(T_{4}^{-}+1)^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2})}\cdot(1-\frac{1\pm o(1)}{1+\exp(\frac{2}{\pi}m_{k}(T_{4}^{-}+1)^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma_{p}^{2}s^{2})})\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{(1\pm o(1))3\sqrt{2}n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}{1.01\sigma_{p}s}\cdot 0.5\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(T_{4}^{-}+1)\eta\sigma_{p}s(1\pm o(1))

\displaystyle\geq\penalty\

\displaystyle 1.4n(T_{4}^{-}+1)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\geq\penalty\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle,

where the first step is by Eq. (
35
),
the second step is by Eq. (
36
), (
37
) and
T_{4}^{-}\geq T_{3}
,
the last step is by the upper bound in Eq. (
24
). Similarly, we can show that for all
s\in[m_{k}]
and
i\in[n]

\displaystyle s_{i,21}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}s_{i,22}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right|\geq 2s_{i,11}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}s_{i,12}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}\left|\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{4}^{-}+1)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle\right|,

which contradicts with the definition of
T_{4}^{-}
. Therefore, we have
T_{4}^{-}\geq\tilde{T}_{4}^{-}
.
∎

Remark E.22
.

Note the constant
0.99
in the definition of
\tilde{T}_{4}^{-}
is crucial and its impact is on the exponent.
When this constant is greater than 1, then the bound does not hold. Furthermore, we have
s_{i,21}^{(t)}\leq 0.1
for
t\geq T_{4}^{-}
.

Next, the following lemmas study the behaviour of query/key signal.

Lemma E.23
.

The query signal are monotonic for
t\in[T_{3},t^{*}]
. Formally, for all
t\in[T_{3},t^{*}]
and
s\in[m_{k}]
,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

where
t^{*}
is defined in Eq. (
31
).

Lemma E.24
.

The key signal are monotonic for
t\in[T_{3},t^{*}]
. Formally, for all
t\in[T_{3},t^{*}]
and
s\in[m_{k}]
,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t+1)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\sgn(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)\eta\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert,

where
t^{*}
is defined in Eq. (
31
).

Proof of Lemma
E.23
.

WLOG, given neuron
s
, suppose
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle>0
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle<0
. For query signal, similar to previous analysis, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}(-\ell_{i}^{\prime(t)}s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)})\cdot\left(-y_{i}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{8\sqrt{\pi}}t\eta\sigma_{p}s(1\pm o(1))\cdot(\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{K,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(T_{2})}_{K,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle+(t-T_{2})(\eta\sigma_{p}s/\sqrt{2}-\eta n\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert)>0,

where the first step is by Eq. (
11
),
the second step is by the concentration of
\ell_{i}^{\prime}
and
s_{i,11}^{(t)}
for
t\leq T_{4}
, i.e., Lemma
E.14
and
E.15
,
the fourth step is by
\mathcal{E}(t)
and the lower bound Eq. (
22
),
and the last step is by lower bound Eq. (
23
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
E.24
.

For key signal, we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{K,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}(-l_{i}^{\prime(t)})\cdot\left(-y_{i}s_{i,11}^{(t)}s_{i,12}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle-s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{v}}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2\sqrt{\pi}}t\eta\sigma_{p}s(1\pm o(1))\cdot(-s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\frac{1}{4}(1\pm o(1))\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i\in[n]}s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle-\frac{1}{4}(1\pm o(1))n\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle)

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i\in[n]}s_{i,21}^{(t)}s_{i,22}^{(t)}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=_{\sgn}

\displaystyle-s_{21}^{(t)}s_{22}^{(t)}(1\pm o(1))\sum_{i\in[n]}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}^{(t)}_{Q,s},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle<0,

where the first step is by Eq. (
11
),
the second step is by the concentration of
\ell_{i}^{\prime}
and
s_{i,11}^{(t)}
for
t\leq T_{4}
, i.e., Lemma
E.14
and
E.15
,
the fourth step is by the definition of
T_{4}^{-}
, i.e., Eq. (
29
),
the fifth step is by the
\mathcal{B}(t)
,
the last step is due to the upper bound Eq. (
23
) and
\mathcal{D}(T_{3}),\dots\mathcal{D}(t-1)
.
∎

At the end of this section, we give an upper bound for
T_{4}^{-}
.

Lemma E.25
.

We have

\displaystyle T_{4}^{-}\leq\hat{T}_{4}^{-}:=\sqrt{\frac{1.01\pi}{2}}\sqrt{\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{p}s}{\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\right\rVert}\right)}\eta^{-1}m_{k}^{-1/2}\sigma_{p}^{-1}s^{-1}.

(38)

Proof.

Suppose
T_{4}^{-}>\hat{T}_{4}^{-}
.
First, we estimate the magnitude of
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},y_{i}\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\xi}}_{i}\rangle
and
\langle\boldsymbol{\mathbf{w}}_{Q,s}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\mathbf{\mu}}\rangle
for
T_{3}\leq t\leq T_{4}^{-}
.
WLOG, given neuron
s
, suppose
